- **Mechanism:** Randomised reward + scarcity urgency + sunk-cost display recruits exactly the impulsive-choice system that meta-analysis shows is atypical in ADHD, and does so with no performance-contingent component to anchor it.
- **Piano-app application:** Trivially satisfied by Fingerly's existing constraints — no backend, no accounts, no monetisation. But generalise the principle beyond money: ban randomised *anything* (random practice-item selection presented as a 'draw', mystery unlocks, surprise badges) and ban all artificial scarcity/urgency ('practise today or lose access to this piece'). The mechanic class is the problem, not the currency.
- **Verifier correction:** The numbers are right; the inference is not. Verified: n = 7,422, eta-squared = 0.054 for loot box spending vs 0.004 for other microtransaction spending, PLOS ONE 13(11) e0206767, correction published 14 March 2019 (e0214167), and the 2019 replication is real — 'Loot boxes are again linked to problem gambling: Results of a replication study', PLOS ONE 14(3) e0213194, n = 1,172 (SMALLER than the original, not larger), eta-squared = 0.051 in a sample that 'did not self-select into a loot box study'. But 'an order-of-magnitude difference ISOLATING THE RANDOMISATION ITSELF as the harmful component' is an unsupported causal leap. Both studies are cross-sectional self-report surveys and the authors state plainly that 'it is impossible to understand the direction of the causal relationship'. Comparing two eta-squared values across two predictors in a correlational survey does not isolate a causal component; problem gamblers plausibly select into randomised spending. Restate as: loot box spending is more strongly associated with problem gambling than non-randomised spending is, replicated, direction of causation unknown.
- **Replication:** This is the one item that earns STRONG, but only for the narrow association. Direct replication by the original authors in 2019 (eta-squared 0.051 vs 0.054, and importantly in a non-self-selected sample, addressing the original's biggest confound). Independent meta-analytic confirmation: Garea, Drummond, Sauer, Hall & Williams (2021), 'Meta-analysis of the relationship between problem gambling, excessive gaming and loot box spending', International Gambling Studies 21(3), 460-479, finds a consistent association across studies. So: association replicated and meta-analytic = STRONG. Causal attribution to randomisation = not established by any of this. Rate the association STRONG and the mechanism claim UNVERIFIED.
- **Verifier notes:** Second and more serious problem: the Skinner framing is the exact folklore this review targets. Variable-ratio schedules producing high steady response rates and strong resistance to extinction is a real operant finding, but its application here is analogical, not measured — no one has demonstrated that any commercial compulsion loop operates on a VR schedule in humans. And it is self-refuting against this very claim set: pity systems (claims 1 and 2) are explicitly NOT variable-ratio. A hard guarantee at pull 90 or summon 120 bounds the ratio and makes the reinforcement increasingly predictable as the counter climbs — the opposite of the schedule Skinner showed maximises persistence. Calling pity-gated gacha a 'Skinner box on a variable-ratio schedule' is precisely wrong. Also: eta-squared = 0.054 is ~5% of variance, a small-to-medium effect; the authors' own defensible phrasing is that it is 'neither small, nor trivial' and larger than some established risk factors, which is a much narrower claim than the one made here. Schüll (2012, Princeton UP) is real and correctly characterised as ethnographic/documentary, not quantitative evidence.

### The strongest evidence that gamification does NOT fix ADHD cognition: EndeavorRx/AKL-T01 obtained FDA clearance on a statistically significant but small change in a lab attention measure, with no significant improvement on the seven secondary parent/clinician rating scales; and a meta-analysis of game-based digital interventions found no significant effects on working memory, inhibition, shifting, planning, or emotional control.

- **Status:** MISCHARACTERIZED · MODERATE
- **Citation:** Kollins SH et al. (2020). A novel digital intervention for actively reducing severity of paediatric ADHD (STARS-ADHD): a randomised controlled trial. The Lancet Digital Health 2(4):e168–e178. + FDA De Novo DEN200026 review. + Meta-analysis of game-based digital interventions for ADHD (8 RCTs), summarised by ADHD Evidence / adhdevidence.org.
- **URL:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33334505/
- **Peer reviewed:** None
- **Sample / design:** Double-blind parallel-group RCT with an active digital control — genuinely good design, which is why the null secondary endpoints matter. The meta-analysis is small and heterogeneous.
- **Effect size:** STARS-ADHD: n = 348 children aged 8–12, 25 min/day × 5 days/week × 4 weeks. Primary endpoint TOVA Attention Performance Index median change 0.88 (95% CI 0.24–1.49, p = 0.006); mean change 0.93 (SD 3.15) vs 0.03 (SD 3.16) control. FDA review notes no statistically meaningful difference on non-parametric analysis of the 7 secondary parental/clinical rating scales. Separate meta-analysis: 8 RCTs (4 at high risk of bias); no significant improvement in working memory/inhibition (4 RCTs, n = 481), shifting (3 RCTs, n = 160), or planning/emotional control (2 RCTs, n = 131); only the two smallest studies (n = 90) showed visuospatial memory gains.
- **Kind:** BOTH
- **Evidence type:** PEER_REVIEWED
- **Ethical status:** NEEDS_CARE
- **Mechanism:** Gamified cognitive training reliably produces improvement on the trained task and its near-transfer lab measure; far transfer to functional behaviour is where it repeatedly fails. Gamification's demonstrated strength is adherence, not mechanism.
- **Piano-app application:** Sets the honest ceiling for Fingerly: game mechanics can plausibly get an ADHD adult to sit down at the piano and stay for 20 minutes, and that is the entire claim. They will not improve attention, and the app should never imply otherwise. Design consequence: every mechanic must serve *doing the actual practice* (real repertoire, real MIDI input) rather than being a parallel mini-game — the transfer problem is exactly what an abstracted training game runs into.
- **Verifier correction:** Part A is confirmed at the strongest possible evidentiary level and should be kept verbatim. Part B is wrong and should be deleted or rewritten. The best-matching 8-RCT meta-analysis is Lee H, Lee S, Hwang M, Woo K (2025), European Child & Adolescent Psychiatry 34(12):3853–3869, PMID 40522458, DOI 10.1007/s00787-025-02788-5 — and it DID find significant effects on visuospatial short-term memory (MD 0.67, 95% CI 0.25–1.10) and visuospatial working memory (MD 0.47, 95% CI 0.05–0.89). Inhibition and monitoring (BRIEF) were null. Planning and emotional control were NOT MEASURED AT ALL. So the claim (i) denies a significant working-memory effect that was found, and (ii) asserts null results for two outcomes that were never tested. Additionally, citing a meta-analysis via a secondary aggregator with no authors, year or journal is not a citable reference — replace it.
- **Replication:** Part A needs no replication argument — it is a regulatory finding of fact from the trial's own submission. Part B is the problem: I could not locate a meta-analysis matching the claim's stated domain list, and the one that matches on design (8 RCTs, game-based, ADHD) contradicts it on working memory. Note the surrounding literature does support the claim's THESIS even where this specific citation fails: Schultz BK, Evans SW, Carter KM et al. (2025), Front Psychiatry 16:1605744, a review of 37 studies, concluded 'far transfer effects are unconvincing for seminal game elements'. Cite that instead.
- **Verifier notes:** I verified Part A against the primary regulatory document, not a summary: I downloaded and parsed the FDA De Novo decision summary for DEN200026 (Akili Interactive Labs, product code QFT, 21 CFR 882.5803, received 16 April 2020). It states verbatim: 'There was no statistically meaningful difference in a[n] analysis of the 7 secondary [paren]tal or clinical rating scales.' The listed secondaries were ADHD-RS Total, Inattentive and Hyperactive subscales, ADHD Impairment Rating Scale, CGI-I, and BRIEF working-memory and inhibit scales. Primary endpoint: TOVA API mean change 0.93 (AKL-T01) vs 0.03 (control), p=0.006, n=348 randomised at 20 US sites. Strongest corroboration of all: the FDA-cleared Indications For Use itself concedes patients 'may not display benefits in typical behavioral symptoms, such as hyperactivity.' Kollins et al. 2020 verifies exactly on journal, volume, pages. One methodological note worth adding: enrolment required a TOVA API baseline below −1.8, and 482 of 857 screened children were excluded largely for not showing a TOVA-defined attention issue — the trial selected on the primary outcome measure, which further limits how much the 0.9-point TOVA change generalises.

### Counter to the intuitive assumption, ADHD adults are not uniformly more manipulable by dark patterns — in a controlled comparison they recognised deceptive designs no worse than non-ADHD participants, avoided some MORE often, and reported lower frustration and effort. The vulnerability is specific (data disclosure under manipulative framing), not global.

- **Status:** CONFIRMED · STRONG
- **Citation:** Stefanidi E et al. (2025). A Comparative Study of How People With and Without ADHD Recognise and Avoid Dark Patterns on Social Media. Proceedings of CHI 2025. arXiv:2503.05263.
- **URL:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05263
- **Peer reviewed:** None
- **Sample / design:** Controlled online experiment with self-reported/screened ADHD status; social-media context, so generalisation to a practice app is partial.
- **Effect size:** n = 135 interactive web study. Overall dark-pattern recognition was low in BOTH groups with no significant between-group difference; ADHD participants avoided certain specific patterns (e.g. premium upselling) more often; ADHD participants reported lower effort, frustration and physical demand; dark patterns raised cognitive load for everyone. Reported ADHD-specific risk: greater willingness to disclose personal data under manipulative framing.
- **Kind:** VISIBLE_UI
- **Evidence type:** PEER_REVIEWED
- **Ethical status:** NEEDS_CARE
- **Mechanism:** Lived experience with attention-capturing interfaces may confer some learned resistance, and the 'ADHD = impulsive = exploitable' story is too coarse. The risk concentrates where the pattern exploits low-friction compliance (accepting defaults, disclosing) rather than sustained persuasion.
- **Piano-app application:** Don't over-correct into a paternalistic UI that strips the user of agency on the theory that they can't be trusted with choices. The evidence supports removing *manipulative* patterns while keeping *real* choices intact and reversible. For Fingerly specifically: make defaults conservative (session length, stimulation intensity, notification frequency) since low-friction default-acceptance is the documented weak point, but keep every one of those defaults visibly and easily overridable in settings — the user should be able to change what the app does, just not while mid-session and not via a one-tap prompt the app itself surfaces.
- **Verifier correction:** Verified directly from the paper (Hum Soc Sci Commun (2025) 12:962, doi 10.1057/s41599-025-05000-w, authors Romain Ghibellini & Beat Meier). Screening produced 'a final sample of 59 publications. Of these, 38 investigated the Zeigarnik effect, 20 investigated the Ovsiankina effect, and 1 investigated both.' Weighted recall ratio = 0.99 including Zeigarnik's own 1927 data (N=38) and 0.99 excluding it (N=37); the authors note Zeigarnik's original computations are inflated. Cohen's dz = 0.15 weighted across N=8 publications. Ovsiankina resumption is robust (individual studies 61-91% resumption). Two small precisions: the units are PUBLICATIONS, not studies; and the authors' conclusion is more nuanced than 'does not replicate' — they argue the Zeigarnik effect may depend on situational influences and individual differences (experimenter authority, situational demands, task involvement) that were common historically and are rare now, concluding it 'lacks universal validity' rather than that it is nonexistent.
- **Replication:** This IS the replication evidence. dz=0.15 with a pooled ratio of 0.99 across ~60 publications is about as close to a null as a literature gets. Netflix 'Continue Watching' and Duolingo lesson prompts are real product patterns; attributing them to resumption + cue availability rather than memory is the claimant's reasoning, not the paper's, but it is consistent with the paper.
- **Verifier notes:** This is the only STRONG item in the set and the one most worth acting on: it is a meta-analysis, it is recent, and it directly retires a piece of folklore that this design area repeats constantly. The claim's own framing — that practitioners citing 'Zeigarnik' for progress UI are usually describing Ovsiankina — is a sound inference and is the useful takeaway.

---

## Highest-leverage conclusion

**Make "how much of this song is done" a finite, visible, monotone count of passages cleared at a REACHABLE criterion, and pay out a named segment of it inside every sitting.** (Invisible mechanic #1 + Visible UI #1, built together.) The 80% cliff is not a metaphor in this codebase — it is `SessionEngine.selectWorkPassage()` (`/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/SessionEngine.kt:265`). Passages are consumed in `difficultyRank` order, so the remaining work is BY CONSTRUCTION the hardest work; marginal cost per unit of progress rises monotonically toward the end. Meanwhile there is no song-completion quantity anywhere in the app (verified: no "N of M passages" string exists), so there is no goal gradient at all — only rising difficulty. And the only terminal reward ("the song is finished") is the most delay-discounted object in the app, which Jackson & MacKillop 2016 (CONFIRMED · STRONG, N=3,913, d=0.43 — the strongest reward finding in the record) says is worth close to nothing to this learner. Rising cost + invisible denominator + a worthless-because-distant payoff is the exact shape that produces abandonment at 80%. Fixing it costs one derived value in `SessionEngine` (`cleared = passages.count { progressOf(it).bestCleanIndex <= SESSION_TARGET_INDEX }`, `total = passages.size`) and one reuse of the existing segmented renderer `drawDrillProgress` (`NoteHighwayView.kt:731`). It adds no reward layer, no currency, no ceremony — which is exactly what Sailer & Homner 2020 (CONFIRMED · STRONG) predicts should be where the effect actually lives. Second-order but inseparable: **define song completion at a rung he can actually reach.** Today `SESSION_TARGET_INDEX = 3` (both hands, 55% tempo) is the mastery gate the engine already uses, but nothing declares it the finish line, so the implicit finish line is "play it like a pianist" — an asymptote. Declaring "song complete = every passage clean at both-hands/55%" and declaring full tempo a separate, explicitly OPTIONAL second ladder converts an unreachable 100% into a reachable one. That single definitional change is the cheapest anti-cliff mechanic available.

- **Tension:** Game-design record: streaks are the single most transferable retention finding (Duolingo, 600+ experiments, Streak Society, streak freeze/repair as the mercy layer). Existing ADHD record: streaks rejected outright on Jackson & MacKillop 2016 (CONFIRMED · STRONG) + Baumel 2019.
  - Resolution: Existing decision UPHELD. The new evidence is strictly weaker than the evidence that killed it: Duolingo's figures are self-published industry retrospectives with no control group, they optimise DAU rather than learning, and Silverman & Barasch is consumer behaviour in a non-ADHD population. ADHD-specific evidence actively points the other way (Carlson & Tamm on response-cost sensitivity; the abstinence-violation dynamic making a break an abandonment trigger). The useful residue — cheap re-entry after a lapse — is already SPEC §3's 'don't miss twice' rule and RESEARCH.md:810's keep-the-gate-open decision. The ledger in this plan is explicitly a COUNT OF WORK DONE, not a count of days, and it can never decrease.
- **Tension:** Lee & Zentall (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:198) asks that the FORM of purely-motivational payoffs not be fully predictable, to fight habituation. The ADHD-gaming record and both rejected-idea lists demand strict determinism: identical performance must always produce identical evaluation and identical reward.
  - Resolution: Resolved by separating NON-REPETITION from UNPREDICTABILITY, and implementing only the former. Celebration identity becomes a pure function of which milestone it is (deterministic, content-indexed, non-repeating); triggers and reward magnitude stay fully deterministic and mastery-gated. This is a stated NARROWING of decision 1.14, not a silent reinterpretation: I am not implementing 'the payoff's form is not fully predicted', I am implementing 'the payoff's form does not repeat'. The ADHD-specific reason to narrow it is Hulsbosch 2023 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE), which found ADHD participants extinguished FASTER under partial reinforcement — so anything that makes delivery itself feel uncertain carries downside risk here that it does not carry in a general population. Kao et al. CHI 2024's report that learnable rather than random variation predicts voluntary playtime independently favours the narrowed version.
- **Tension:** Invisible mechanic #6 inserts a consolidation rep after 2 consecutive sub-criterion reps. The record explicitly REJECTED 'bucket sessions into 5-minute blocks and trigger an activity switch when accuracy, timing SD or miss rate degrades past a personal noise band' (RESEARCH.md:899).
  - Resolution: Different mechanic, and the difference is load-bearing. The rejected proposal was (a) TIME-bucketed, (b) a fatigue CLAIM resting on a direct Tucha-vs-Fuermaier conflict in the adult time-on-task literature, (c) required building and tuning a new detector with a personal noise band, and (d) switched ACTIVITY. This proposal is (a) not time-based at all, (b) makes no fatigue claim, (c) consumes the exact accuracy signal at the exact threshold `AutoDifficulty.adjust()` already uses (`STEP_DOWN_BELOW = 70f`) with no new detector, and (d) does not switch activity — same loop, same input model, same scoring, different material, which is specifically required by King et al. 2007 on task-switching cost in adult ADHD. It is damped to 2 consecutive observations per the record's own no-single-trial-state-change rule (Aster/Sethi) and capped at 2 inserts per sitting.
- **Tension:** SPEC §6 lists 'combo counter' in the always-on micro tier and promises a 'full-screen WoW-style takeover' whose soundtrack is a multi-second playback of the user's own recording. The record bans streaks (RESEARCH.md:881), bans deducting a previously-earned metric (RESEARCH.md:244), bans live evaluative feedback during play (Gabay 2018), and requires all §6 takeovers to be interruptible by the next keypress (RESEARCH.md:220) — a clause SPEC §6 does not contain.
  - Resolution: SPEC §6 amended in two places, stated openly rather than worked around. (1) 'Combo counter (constant)' → 'longest clean run, post-rep only'. `HitJudge.combo` resets on every extra, miss and out-of-window press, making the live display simultaneously a streak, a deducted metric and a per-note verdict — three separate existing prohibitions. `bestCombo` within a rep is monotone and pays out inside the sitting, so it survives as a post-rep diagnostic. (2) The milestone takeover's own-recording playback becomes an OFFERED action on the finish screen rather than an automatic multi-second takeover, and every celebration gains a hard interruptibility clause. Neither amendment overturns a research finding — both resolve SPEC text that predates the research record against findings already accepted in it.
- **Tension:** The strongest reward-side design guidance in the record (RESEARCH.md:152) says to spend the engineering budget on making the APPROACH legible via continuous proximity feedback rather than on making the payoff bigger — but it rests on Plichta & Scheres, tagged MISCHARACTERIZED · WEAK, which under RESEARCH.md:7 cannot drive a parameter change.
  - Resolution: The proximity/progress displays in this plan are rejustified on admissible evidence and reframed accordingly. The song completion bar and the per-rep remaining-cost bar are COST DISCLOSURES, not reward-anticipation devices, and they stand on Chong et al. 2023 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE — perceived cost at initiation is the lever, state the effort quantum) plus Jackson & MacKillop (CONFIRMED · STRONG — the payout must land inside the sitting, which requires a countable per-sitting unit). This matters for what gets built: a cost-disclosure bar is honest at any magnitude and needs no ceremony attached, whereas an anticipation device would want a ramp and a reveal — which is exactly what the weak evidence cannot license.
- **Tension:** Left 4 Dead's AI Director and Candy Crush's difficulty oscillation are the two most attractive borrows in the game-design set, and both are engagement controllers by construction. Papoušek et al. 2016 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE) says within-session engagement proxies systematically select for too-easy practice — engagement and learning point in opposite directions.
  - Resolution: Adopt the pacing SHAPE, refuse the control SIGNAL. The sawtooth (hard work → consolidation → end on mastered material) is imported; the Director's actual input — 'has this player had enough recently' — is not. The only permitted input to any pacing or difficulty decision remains measured accuracy on the current rep, and invisible mechanic #3 makes that structural by locking `AutoDifficulty.adjust()`'s signature behind a test and building the missing reference-attempt probe so tuning happens against next-session cold performance. Concretely this means the boss chapter's difficulty may never be tuned on 'did he finish the boss', which will feel like the obvious signal.
- **Tension:** Optimal-stimulation theory (Zentall) says added task-irrelevant stimulation improves ADHD performance on low-interest tasks, implying the particle/glow budget should be generous. Kao 2020 finds an inverted-U where Extreme juiciness loses to Medium on performance, motivation AND playtime.
  - Resolution: Neither drives a parameter; both inform a control. The verifier found all three optimal-stimulation citations to be theory papers presented as empirical evidence, with the framework substantially superseded and its live descendant (white-noise/stochastic-resonance) carrying a mixed replication record — so 'clutter is functional for ADHD' is not established. Kao 2020 is UNCHECKED. The admissible basis is Nigg et al. 2024 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:277), which supports moderate stimulation AND carries the rider that calibration is personal and intensity should be adjustable — a rider SPEC §6 currently ignores. Hence: a 0–3 control defaulting to 2, rather than a tuned constant in either direction.
- **Tension:** Kao et al. CHI 2024 (pre-registered, n=1,699) is the strongest causal evidence in the visible-juice set — amplified feedback firing on mere action REDUCED competence (−0.43), while the same feedback gated on success raised it (+0.45) — but it is tagged UNCHECKED and therefore below the record's change bar.
  - Resolution: Not used to drive anything new. Its two actionable conclusions are already required by CONFIRMED evidence already in the record: success-contingency is Hulsbosch 2023 (keep the micro tier continuous AND contingent), and the current code already satisfies it — `spawnBurst` fires only when `judge.onNoteOn()` returns a hit index (NoteHighwayView.kt:259-261), and a wrong key gets only a red flash with no burst. The occlusion guard (invisible mechanic #5) is therefore justified as maintenance of the EXISTING contingency requirement under high note density, with CHI 2024 cited as converging support and explicitly not as the driver. Worth adopting from it regardless, as a measurement discipline: judge any juice change by voluntary practice minutes after the required block ends, never by self-reported enjoyment.

## Changes this evidence produced

- The 80% cliff is a specific line of code, not a personality trait. `SessionEngine.selectWorkPassage()` (SessionEngine.kt:265) picks the lowest-mastery passage in `difficultyRank` order, so passages are consumed easiest-first and the remaining 20% is by construction the hardest 20%. Combined with a completely invisible denominator (no 'N of M passages' exists anywhere in the app) and a terminal reward that delay-discounting says is worth ~nothing, this is the precise mechanical shape that produces quitting near the end. Every other item on this list is smaller than fixing that.
- Almost every game-design borrow that survives contact with the ADHD record turns out to be a SCHEDULING or INFORMATION-STRUCTURE change, not a reward change. The record already predicted this: Sailer & Homner 2020 (CONFIRMED · STRONG) found the cognitive effect of gamification (g=.49) held under methodological rigour while the motivational (g=.36) and behavioural (g=.25) effects did not survive. This plan's ratio is deliberate — 6 invisible mechanics and 8 visible items, of which 3 visible items are removals or honesty fixes rather than additions.
- The single largest available REWARD improvement is a deletion. `SessionScreen.kt` never sets `showHud`, so it inherits `true` from `NoteHighwayView.kt:114` — meaning real song practice still renders `acc N% hit N miss N extra N combo N` plus developer frame telemetry (`120Hz fps 119 dropped 0 particles 37`) every frame. That is a live evaluative HUD (Gabay 2018), a loss-framed falling percentage (Aster 2024), and a resettable streak that deducts a previously-earned metric — three decisions the project already made and justified, then shipped only to the foundations path (FoundationsScreen.kt:135).
- Where the game-design literature and the ADHD record disagree about unpredictability, the correct resolution is 'non-repetition, not unpredictability.' Lee & Zentall (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, in-record) wanted the FORM of motivational payoffs not to be fully predictable, to fight habituation. The ADHD record demands strict determinism (identical performance must always produce identical evaluation and reward). Both are satisfied by making celebration identity a pure function of WHICH milestone it is — deterministic, content-indexed, non-repeating. Kao et al. CHI 2024 independently reports that learnable variation, not random variation, is what predicts voluntary time-on-task, which is converging support rather than a second requirement.
- Two hot-path defects make any juice work unsafe before it starts. `markLandmarkGroup` (NoteHighwayView.kt:652) executes `intArrayOf(1,3)` / `intArrayOf(6,8,10)` inside `drawNotes` on every frame a revealed recall prompt is on screen — a per-frame heap allocation in the render loop, which CLAUDE.md classes as a bug outright. And `updateParticles` is fed `frameIntervalNanos / 1e9` (NoteHighwayView.kt:493) — the NOMINAL refresh period, not the measured delta — so bursts play in slow motion exactly when frames are dropping, i.e. when the artifact is most visible. Under SPEC §1 these are release blockers, not polish.
- The most compulsion-prone object in this plan is the boss chapter, and the mitigation has to be structural rather than a warning label. Concretely: boss sittings get SHORTER (workMs 9min → 6min), not longer; no timer, no expiry, no streak; abandonable mid-chapter with every cleared segment retained; and Rohrer & Taylor's existing refuse-extra-reps rule stays in force so the boss cannot become a grind. Lee & Zentall additionally predicts a performance dip late in any single reward context — which is exactly where a boss chapter sits — so extending the sitting there is the precise wrong move.
- Honest ceiling, worth stating in the SPEC: the strongest evidence in the entire corpus (EndeavorRx/Kollins 2020 + the null game-based-intervention meta-analysis, and Sailer & Homner) supports one claim only — these mechanics can plausibly get him to sit down and stay for twenty minutes. They will not improve attention, and no copy in the app should imply otherwise. Every mechanic must therefore serve doing the actual practice on the actual MIDI piano, never a parallel mini-game, because far transfer is exactly where abstracted training games repeatedly fail.

### [P0] Song completion ledger + a REACHABLE definition of 'song complete'

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/SessionEngine.kt` — add a pure derived value `fun ledger(): Ledger` returning `cleared = passages.count { progressOf(it).bestCleanIndex <= PassageProgress.SESSION_TARGET_INDEX }`, `total = passages.size`, `bossFromId = ceil(total * 0.8).toInt()`. Declare in code and in SPEC §4 that **song complete = every passage clean at `SESSION_TARGET_INDEX` (both hands, 55% tempo)**; full tempo (ladder index 0) becomes a separate, explicitly optional second ladder that is never presented as unfinished business. `Decomposer.decompose` already emits passages in song order with sequential `id` (Decomposer.kt:42-51), so both the positional boss set and the completion fraction are computable today with zero new data. Persist nothing new — `bestCleanIndex` is already in `PassageProgress` and already round-trips through `SessionRepository`.
- **ADHD adaptation:** The neurotypical version of this is a progress bar for motivation (goal gradient). The ADHD version is a bar for COST DISCLOSURE: the denominator is fixed, can never grow mid-song, and cleared segments can never un-clear (monotone, gain-framed — Aster et al. 2024, RESEARCH.md:244). It also deliberately refuses the endowed-progress trick of pre-crediting the bar; the count is real cleared passages only. And the completion criterion is set at a rung the learner can actually reach in weeks rather than years, because an asymptotic finish line is the thing that makes 80% permanent.
- **Rationale:** Jackson & MacKillop 2016 (CONFIRMED · STRONG, N=3,913, d=0.43) is the strongest reward finding in the record and already drives decision 1.4 ('every reward collectible inside the current session'). A song-completion reward is the most delayed object in the app; the only way the last 20% carries any motivational value is if it decomposes into a countable, finite denominator that advances inside each sitting. Chong et al. 2023 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE) supplies the other half: perceived cost at the decision to start is the lever, and an unbounded remaining cost is the maximally aversive case. The regulatory principle from the kompu-gacha literature generalises cleanly and honestly here: never present an activity whose total cost-to-completion is unknowable — always show the full remaining path.
- **Risk:** Low. This displays measured state that already exists. The one hazard is the temptation to make the denominator flattering by counting easier rungs — do not; it must be the same `SESSION_TARGET_INDEX` the engine already uses to decide what to practise, or the displayed number diverges from the internal one (the XCOM failure mode).

### [P1] Boss chapter as a per-sitting ladder with SHORTER sittings, not an end-of-song payoff

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/SessionEngine.kt` — when every remaining un-cleared passage has `id >= ledger().bossFromId`, enter boss mode: (a) `selectWorkPassage()` switches from global `difficultyRank` order to song order within the boss set, so the chapter can be performed end-to-end; (b) `workMs` drops from 9min to ~6min; (c) `enterVictory()` becomes mandatory at the end of every boss sitting rather than time-gated, so each boss sitting still ends on already-mastered material; (d) each boss sitting produces its own named finish state carrying the ledger delta. The existing Rohrer & Taylor refuse-extra-reps rule stays in force inside the boss. No new phase enum is strictly required — a `bossActive: Boolean` on the existing WORK phase is enough.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Mainstream boss design extends the session and escalates stakes at the climax. This inverts both: shorter sittings, lower stakes per sitting, more finish states. The chapter is fully abandonable at any point with every cleared segment retained forever — no decay, no catch-up debt, no 'welcome back' (RESEARCH.md:429/810, Baumel et al.). Difficulty inside the chapter still rides the same `AutoDifficulty` ladder, so 'never hit a wall' is preserved.
- **Rationale:** SPEC §2.7 already mandates the boss chapter ('the final 20% of each song is explicitly gamified as a completion boss fight') — this item does not justify it, it SPECIFIES it so it doesn't violate the evidence. Jackson & MacKillop (CONFIRMED · STRONG) forces per-sitting payout. Lee & Zentall (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:198) predicts a performance dip late in any single reward context — the boss sits exactly there, so the sitting must be shortened, not extended. Candy Crush's documented hard/light level oscillation and Left 4 Dead's Relax state (both CONFIRMED · MODERATE industry engineering) are the source of the mandatory end-on-mastered-material rule: an easy item after a hard one converts relief into momentum and re-establishes the contrast the next hard item needs.
- **Risk:** HIGH on the compulsion axis — this is the single most compulsion-prone item in the plan, and the ADHD/gaming-disorder association (Koncz et al. 2023, CONFIRMED · STRONG) is the base rate it deploys into. Mandatory mitigations, all structural: no countdown, no expiry, no daily availability window, no streak, no 'finish tonight' framing, and honest wall-clock overrun logged and surfaced afterward (RESEARCH.md:416). If a boss sitting ever runs long, the app should say so in numbers, not congratulate.

### [P1] Codify the anti-engagement rule: nothing may tune difficulty against within-session engagement, and build the missing reference-attempt probe

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/AutoDifficulty.kt` — `adjust()` currently takes only `(index, lastIndex, accuracyPercent)`, which is correct; lock that in with a unit test in `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/test/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/` asserting the controller's inputs, plus a comment forbidding session-completion, notes-played, reps-logged, boss-finished or 'did he keep going' from ever becoming inputs. Then build the missing probe: in `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/data/SessionRepository.kt`, flag the FIRST attempt on a passage in each new session, at its unmodified resume rung, as `isReference = true`. Tune everything (ladder thresholds, boss ladder, drill triggers) against next-session reference-attempt pass rates only.
- **ADHD adaptation:** This is the mechanism-level version of the user's own stated goal ('keep me occupied and learning more' — not maximised time-on-app). It is also the ADHD-specific safety valve: Koncz et al. 2023 says 'this app is engaging' is a risk signal here, not a success metric, so the difficulty controller must be structurally incapable of chasing it.
- **Rationale:** Papoušek, Stanislav & Pelánek 2016 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:639) is the load-bearing anti-gamification finding in the whole record: within-session engagement proxies systematically select for too-easy practice, i.e. engagement and learning point in opposite directions. The record already states this and explicitly notes the reference-attempt rows are NOT yet computed — so this is finishing a decision, not proposing one. It is also the structural guarantee that everything else in this plan cannot quietly become an engagement optimiser.
- **Risk:** None to the user. The cost is that a boss chapter's difficulty cannot be tuned on 'did he finish the boss' — which will feel like the obvious signal and must be refused.

### [P0] Fix the two hot-path defects that make any juice work unsafe

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/highway/NoteHighwayView.kt`: (1) hoist the two `intArrayOf(...)` literals out of `markLandmarkGroup` (line 652, called from `drawNotes` line 576) into two `private val` fields — this is a per-frame heap allocation in the render loop; (2) feed `updateParticles` the MEASURED frame delta clamped to ~2 frames instead of `frameIntervalNanos / 1_000_000_000f` (line 493), so bursts stay wall-clock consistent when frames drop; (3) hoist the `System.nanoTime()` call out of `whiteKeyPaintFor`/`blackKeyPaintFor` (lines 663, 674) into one `frameNanos` field set in `doFrame` — it currently runs 88× per frame.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Not ADHD-specific — this is the precondition for everything else. Worth noting only that the <15ms budget in SPEC §1 is simultaneously the reward-delay budget (Jackson & MacKillop, CONFIRMED · STRONG), so latency work and reward work are the same work here.
- **Rationale:** CLAUDE.md and SPEC §1: 'any per-frame allocation in the render loop or MIDI pipeline is a bug'; 'visible jank is a release blocker.' Beyond the letter of the rule, Ryan, Rigby & Przybylski 2006 tie perceived COMPETENCE to control intuitiveness and input latency, and Kaaresoja's latency work finds jitter hurts more than constant lag because the motor system can calibrate to a fixed delay but cannot predict a variable one. A celebration layer built on a jittery substrate is worse than no celebration layer.
- **Risk:** None. Pure fixes. Verify against the existing Phase 2 acceptance gate: zero dropped frames at 120Hz with 200 simultaneous particles.

### [P2] Density-aware burst budget (occlusion guard) on the particle layer

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/highway/NoteHighwayView.kt` — `spawnBurst` (line 686) currently always emits `HIT_BURST = 24` (line 824) with a 0.5–1.0s lifetime. Track `lastHitNanos` and scale the count down (24 → ~8) when the inter-onset interval since the previous judged hit is shorter than the burst lifetime, so a run of sixteenths does not merge into one indistinguishable cloud. Pure arithmetic on existing fields; no allocation; also shortens `plife` proportionally so effect lifetime never exceeds inter-onset interval.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Standard advice would say more feedback is better for ADHD (optimal-stimulation theory). That literature is theory-only and its live descendant has a mixed replication record — the verifier flagged all three optimal-stimulation citations as theory papers presented as empirical evidence. The defensible ADHD-specific requirement is the opposite and narrower: feedback must remain per-note LEGIBLE, because per-note legibility is the whole reason the micro tier exists.
- **Rationale:** Driven by the existing CONFIRMED · MODERATE decision, not by new evidence: Hulsbosch et al. 2023 (RESEARCH.md:187) requires the micro tier to be reliably continuous and CONTINGENT — and a burst that visually merges with its neighbours has stopped being a per-note contingent signal, so the requirement is violated by density even though the code path is unchanged. Kao et al. CHI 2024's occlusion result (pre-registered, n=1,699: feedback that visually engulfs the success signal severs outcome binding and REDUCES competence) is converging support only — it is tagged UNCHECKED and is not load-bearing here. Secondary benefit: 24 particles per note in a dense run pushes toward the 200-particle perf gate.
- **Risk:** Low. Do not let this become adaptive-by-performance — the trigger must be note density in the chart, which is a property of the material, not of how well he is doing.

### [P2] Scheduling shape: accuracy-triggered consolidation inserts, and stratified bag-shuffle for the review queue

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/SessionEngine.kt` — (a) add a `consecutiveSubCriterion` counter in `Phase.WORK`; after 2 consecutive reps below `AutoDifficulty.STEP_DOWN_BELOW` (70%), insert exactly one rep of already-cleared material at `bestCleanIndex + 1` before returning to the work passage, capped at 2 inserts per sitting. (b) `enterReview()` (line 283) currently does `.sortedBy { dueAtMs }.take(3)`, which can serve three adjacent passages from the same region and can indefinitely starve a passage whose due date keeps losing — replace with a pre-allocated `IntArray` bag of due ids, Fisher-Yates shuffled in place in `:core`, then reject a pick whose `startMeasure` neighbours the previous pick when an alternative exists.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Critical constraint from King et al. 2007 (inefficient task-switching in adult ADHD): the consolidation insert must NOT be a mode change. Same loop, same input model, same UI layout, same scoring rules — only the material differs. This is why it is an insert of ordinary already-cleared repertoire rather than a rest screen or a different exercise type. Also damped per Aster/Sethi: it requires 2 consecutive sub-criterion reps, never a single-trial trigger.
- **Rationale:** (a) Left 4 Dead's Director Relax state and King's documented hard/light level alternation (both CONFIRMED · MODERATE industry engineering) both enforce a sawtooth rather than a monotone difficulty line, on the reasoning that sustained high arousal saturates and the trough is what makes the next peak land. (b) Tetris's 7-bag (CONFIRMED · MODERATE) mathematically caps drought length; Spotify's stratified shuffle (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, first-party engineering disclosure) is the same idea applied to perceived variety. Both are explicitly compatible with the ADHD record's line: randomness in WHAT you practise is fine and often good pedagogy; randomness in WHETHER you get credit is prohibited.
- **Risk:** Must not be read as reinstating the REJECTED fatigue detector (RESEARCH.md:899) — see reconciliations. Also, the shuffle must never touch evaluation or reward magnitude; if any 'roll' or reveal animation ever appears around it, that is the tell that it has gone wrong.

### [P0] Song completion bar + a finish label that names the segment that advanced (and reports the BEST clean pass, not the last)

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/SessionScreen.kt` — render the ledger from invisible-mechanic #1 on `BriefingContent` and `FinishedContent` as a segmented bar (reuse the renderer shape of `NoteHighwayView.drawDrillProgress`, line 731): one segment per passage, filled = cleared at criterion, boss segments distinguished by a luminance step rather than a hue change. Copy is a single blunt line: `Gymnopédie 1 — 9 of 14 passages clean @ 55% both hands · boss: 3 left`. Separately fix `SessionEngine.recordFinishCandidate` (line 335), which overwrites unconditionally and therefore stores the LAST clean pass despite its own comment claiming it keeps 'the most impressive' — track the minimum ladder index instead, and append the ledger delta: `Bars 9–12 · both hands · 70% tempo · clean ✓ · 10 of 14`.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Placement is the ADHD-specific decision: this bar goes on the BRIEFING and FINISH surfaces, never on the highway during a rep. Gabay et al. 2018 found adults with ADHD learned WORSE under immediate evaluative feedback (r = −0.38 to −0.39 with symptom severity) — a between-reps completion count is not per-note evaluative, but putting it in the play surface would drift toward the condition that finding rules out. Monotone and gain-framed throughout (Aster et al. 2024): segments fill, never empty.
- **Rationale:** The visible half of the highest-leverage change; see that item for the Jackson & MacKillop / Chong basis. The `recordFinishCandidate` fix is separately mandatory on the XCOM principle (CONFIRMED · MODERATE): never let a displayed number differ from the internally computed one. `finishLabel()` is the app's single most load-bearing motivational string and it currently misreports the session's best result.
- **Risk:** Low. Do not add a percentage — a count of discrete passages is harder to game and easier to act on than a percentage, and it avoids a figure that can appear to fall.

### [P0] Turn off the live evaluative HUD on the song path (and retire the combo counter as a live display)

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/SessionScreen.kt:179-193` — the `NoteHighwayView` built for real reps never sets `showHud`, so it inherits `true` from `NoteHighwayView.kt:114`. Set `showHud = false`, exactly as `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/FoundationsScreen.kt:135` already does. Song reps then get `drawDrillProgress` instead, which is the Chong-justified remaining-cost display. Keep `showHud = true` only for `HighwayScreen` free-play and the perf-test path. `judge.combo` stays as an internal measurement; `judge.bestCombo` may appear in the POST-rep diagnosis line only (it is monotone within a rep and pays out inside the sitting), never live.
- **ADHD adaptation:** The neurotypical rhythm-game default is a permanent live combo/accuracy HUD. Here it is precisely inverted: the ADHD evidence says the evaluative channel must be DELAYED relative to the sensorimotor channel, so the play surface carries only sensorimotor feedback (<15ms particle/key response) plus a non-evaluative cost bar, and all diagnostics arrive after the rep.
- **Rationale:** Not a new proposal — this finishes a decision already made and already justified, which shipped only to foundations. Gabay et al. 2018 (immediate evaluative feedback impairs adult ADHD learning) plus Aster et al. 2024 (a visibly falling accuracy percent is loss-framed). It simultaneously resolves the record's own flagged contradiction that the combo counter is (a) a streak, banned at RESEARCH.md:881, (b) a deducted previously-earned metric, banned at RESEARCH.md:244, and (c) live per-note verdict. It also stops dev telemetry (`120Hz fps 119 dropped 0 particles 37`) rendering during real practice.
- **Risk:** None. This is a deletion. Note it requires an explicit SPEC §6 amendment, since §6 lists 'combo counter' in the always-on micro tier — see reconciliations.

### [P1] Mark the state transitions that currently produce nothing at all

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/FoundationsScreen.kt` and `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/session/FoundationsTrainer.kt` — four of the most expensive-to-earn events in the app are currently silent: `daysCredited++` (FoundationsTrainer.kt:360), `mastered()` flipping true (line 104), `st.rung++` (line 382), and `songGateOpen()` flipping true (FoundationsScreen.kt:112, which opens the entire song side via a silent `prefs.edit()`). Give each one a deterministic ≤400ms marker on the row that changed, plus one blunt line stating the measurement: `C: mastered — 3/3 unaided, 3 distinct days.` No takeover, no full-screen event, no praise. Same treatment for `SessionEngine`'s invisible ladder promotion at `cleanStreak >= 2` (line 165) — state the delta: `rung 6 → 5 (70% tempo, weak hand)`.
- **ADHD adaptation:** This is the highest ratio of ADHD benefit to engineering cost in the whole plan, because it converts already-earned, already-measured events into immediate in-sitting evidence. It is also the version of 'reward' the record permits: informational, unannounced, competence-conveying, delivered as data — which is the exact form Deci, Koestner & Ryan 1999 found does NOT undermine intrinsic motivation, as opposed to expected tangible contingent rewards (d ≈ −0.40).
- **Rationale:** Sailer & Homner 2020 (CONFIRMED · STRONG) — the cognitive effect of gamification survives rigour precisely because gamified conditions add feedback and retrieval structure, while the motivational layer does not survive; therefore spend on INFORMATION STRUCTURE, not celebration magnitude, and an unmarked state change is missing information. Jackson & MacKillop (CONFIRMED · STRONG) — `daysCredited` requires a cold correct first attempt on a distinct day, the most expensive event in the app, and it currently pays nothing at the moment it happens, which is the worst possible position on the delay axis. Kluger & DeNisi 1996 sharpens the copy rule: the marker attaches to a TASK event named in task terms, never to a personal attribute.
- **Risk:** Low, but the copy is where this fails. One sentence, blunt, ≤3 sentences ceiling per SPEC §2.4. The moment it says 'Nice!' it becomes the thing SPEC §2.8 bans and Kluger & DeNisi says carries a ~1-in-3 chance of harming the next run.

### [P1] Post-rep reveal: ~200ms hold, ≤400ms count-up, aborted instantly by the next keypress

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/highway/NoteHighwayView.kt` (the `onEnded` path, line 486) and `SessionScreen`'s briefing transition — on rep end, hold ~200ms, then resolve the metric line with a ≤400ms count-up. ANY MIDI note-on or screen tap during the sequence snaps it to its final state immediately and never blocks input. Also amend SPEC §6's milestone tier: the 'full-screen WoW-style takeover with the user's own recorded performance as the soundtrack' becomes an OFFERED action (a button on the finish screen), not an automatic multi-second takeover — the mechanism already exists in the dashboard's then-vs-now playback.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Standard game design treats the reveal as the reward and stretches it (Overwatch shakes for ~2s before showing anything). Here it is capped hard and made abortable, because dead time is a documented aversive event for this user and because a multi-second animation is time-to-payoff added to a system that discounts delay steeply. The anticipation-building layer is deliberately NOT built up: the record's Plichta anticipation finding is MISCHARACTERIZED · WEAK and the verifier notes the reverse inference is broken, so no ceremony can be justified on it.
- **Rationale:** Low et al. 2018 (in-record, MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:220) — in-app waiting is a first-class performance budget equal to the 15ms keypress budget, and 'celebration animation the user cannot skip' is named explicitly as an aversive event; the record already states all §6 takeovers must be interruptible, and SPEC §6 currently contains no such clause anywhere. Response-time band guidance stable for ~55 years (Miller 1968 / Card / Nielsen; Material Design 200–300ms) puts a state-change animation in the 200–300ms band and says nothing in an interactive loop should occupy the >1s band. The loot-box reveal literature's <500ms anticipation ceiling is used ONLY as an upper bound, never as a reason to add ceremony — its source form is tagged DARK_PATTERN.
- **Risk:** Low, but there is one absolute prohibition: never run the success sequence on a poor result. That is the losses-disguised-as-wins pattern (Dixon et al. 2010) and it is the single most dangerous thing that could be built into a practice app, because it directly corrupts the learning signal the app exists to provide. If the numbers went down, the screen says the numbers went down.

### [P1] Timing-error variability as a displayed metric — and never a rewarded one

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/core/src/main/kotlin/com/fingerly/core/play/HitJudge.kt` — it already accumulates `errMs[]`, `sumAbsErrMs` and `sumSignedErrMs`; add a running SD or IQR over hits (Welford's algorithm, scalar accumulators, zero allocation) and expose it alongside `avgAbsErrorMs()`. Surface it in `Diagnosis.of` (`SessionEngine.kt:349`) and on the dashboard. Separately, per RESEARCH.md:317, use trimmed/robust statistics in the mastery estimator so one lapse does not reset a passage.
- **ADHD adaptation:** This is the clearest display/reward split in the plan and it exists because of an ADHD-specific finding. A neurotypical design would happily gamify consistency. Here consistency is the one axis where effort demonstrably does not move the number, so attaching reward to it manufactures exactly the unfixable-failure experience that ends practice.
- **Rationale:** Kofler et al. 2013 (CONFIRMED · **STRONG**, 319 studies, g = 0.46) — reaction-time variability is the ADHD signature effect and the record already asks for it as a first-class displayed metric; it is currently unbuilt. Marx et al. 2013 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:266) supplies the hard limit: RT variability did NOT respond to reward, so no metric or reward may be built around consistency-of-latency and expect him to be able to move it. Display it, never gate on it, never celebrate it.
- **Risk:** Low, provided the display is framed as measurement rather than as a target. It should sit next to accuracy and tempo without a threshold line drawn on it.

### [P2] State the effort quantum on every start button

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/SessionScreen.kt` — `stepLabel()` currently reports bars, hand, tempo and first note but not the size of the commitment. Append the measured quantum: `Bars 9–12 · left hand · 55% tempo · 14 notes · ~40s`. Same on the boss chapter entry: `Boss: 3 passages left · ~2 sittings at your measured rate` (derived from actual per-rep durations already recorded, never a guess). Foundations already does this (`drillLength = 8 (~60s)`); the song side does not.
- **ADHD adaptation:** The neurotypical framing would sell the payoff ('unlock the next section!'). This sells the cheapness of the cost, because for this learner the binding constraint is effort-cost sensitivity at initiation, not payoff magnitude. It is also why the '2-minute rescue session' in SPEC §3 is worth building — making the smallest viable unit legitimate directly lowers the cost side.
- **Rationale:** Chong, Fortunato & Bellgrove 2023 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE) — the decision to start is where the aversion bites, and the design lever is perceived COST, not reward size; the record already encodes this as decision 1.6 and marks it only partially built. The corollary the record also states and which is currently unbuilt: when auto-difficulty shrinks a passage, SHOW that the cost just dropped ('now 2 bars, right hand only') rather than silently substituting a different label.
- **Risk:** The estimate must be derived from his own recorded rep durations, never from a designer's guess, or it becomes an unverified claim in an app whose value is verified claims.

### [P2] Deterministic, content-indexed rotation of celebration identity (non-repetition, not unpredictability)

- **Change:** New `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/highway/Juice.kt` — a pure `fun celebrationVariant(milestoneIndex: Int): Int` plus a `burstStyle: Int` on `NoteHighwayView`. Milestone TRIGGERS stay exactly where they are: deterministic, earned, mastery-gated (`bestCleanIndex` crossing `SESSION_TARGET_INDEX`, ledger segments, boss chapter entry). Only the visual identity varies, as a pure function of WHICH milestone it is. Implementation: add a parallel `pstyle: IntArray(MAX_PARTICLES)` to the existing SoA pool and 3–4 pre-allocated colour values written to `particlePaint.color` per particle in `drawParticles` — a field write, no allocation, no new draw calls.
- **ADHD adaptation:** This is a deliberate NARROWING of the record's decision 1.14, stated openly: I am implementing non-repetition, not unpredictability. The ADHD-specific reason is that Hulsbosch et al. 2023 found the partial-reinforcement extinction effect did not protect ADHD participants — they made FEWER responses during extinction — so any mechanic that makes reward delivery itself uncertain risks faster extinction, which is the opposite of the goal.
- **Rationale:** Reconciles two CONFIRMED · MODERATE findings that appear to conflict. Lee & Zentall (RESEARCH.md:198 — the only adult continuous-vs-partial evidence in the record) asks that the FORM of purely-motivational payoffs not be fully predictable, to avoid habituation. Sethi et al. (RESEARCH.md:232) endorses rotating celebration-tier visual identity but requires it be mastery-gated, never freely browsable — which the trigger design satisfies. The ADHD record demands strict determinism of evaluation and reward. Content-indexed rotation satisfies all three: identical performance always yields identical evaluation and identical reward magnitude; only the form differs, and it differs legibly. Kao et al. CHI 2024's finding that learnable (not random) variation predicts voluntary time-on-task is converging support.
- **Risk:** Moderate design-drift risk. The line to hold: the rotation must be indexed by MILESTONE identity, not by error class (per-error-class particle colours were explicitly rejected at RESEARCH.md:891 and this must not become a backdoor), and never by `random`. `NoteHighwayView` already holds `Random(42)` — it must not be used for anything the learner can see as a reward.

### [P2] Juice intensity as a real, adjustable setting (0–3, default 2), tunable on the latency screen

- **Change:** `/home/user/fingerly/app/src/main/java/com/fingerly/app/ui/SettingsScreen.kt` — a `juiceIntensity` preference scaling `HIT_BURST` (NoteHighwayView.kt:824) and any glow. Default 2 of 3, not 3. Make the existing in-app latency test screen double as the juice-tuning surface, since it already renders the highway under load and already ships in release builds.
- **ADHD adaptation:** Two ADHD-specific constraints. (1) Stefanidi et al. 2025 (CHI) found ADHD adults are NOT globally more manipulable by dark patterns — the documented weak point is low-friction default acceptance — so the correct posture is a conservative default that is visibly and easily overridable, not a paternalistic lock. (2) The optimum varies day to day and with medication state, which is precisely why it must be a control rather than a tuned constant. But it must not be changeable mid-rep: SPEC §2 bans decision menus before playing, and King et al. 2007 says mid-session mode switching carries an executive cost this user pays at a worse rate.
- **Rationale:** Nigg et al. 2024 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, RESEARCH.md:277) is the record's only genuine endorsement of the §6 'juice is functional, not cosmetic' premise — moderate extra stimulation genuinely aids ADHD performance — and it carries an explicit rider the spec does not honour: the calibration is personal, so intensity should be adjustable. It is currently not adjustable at all. Kao 2020's inverted-U (Medium and High juiciness beat both None and Extreme on performance, motivation and play time) is converging context only, tagged UNCHECKED, and is the reason the default is 2 rather than 3.
- **Risk:** Low. Do not tie intensity to performance (an 'intensity rises as you do well' system is a reward-magnitude variable and reintroduces the thing decision 1.13 forbids).

---

## Ideas rejected on evidence

Kept deliberately: re-proposing these without new evidence is a regression, not an improvement.

### Cross-day streak counter, streak freezes, streak repair (the Duolingo pattern), including 'rolling 30-day density' variants

Already rejected in the record (RESEARCH.md:879, LEARNING.md) on Jackson & MacKillop 2016 (CONFIRMED · STRONG, N=3,913) plus the mental-health-app retention collapse (Baumel 2019, median 30-day retention 3.3%): a streak is a delayed reward whose loss is felt immediately — all downside. The new game-design material does not clear the bar to overturn it. Duolingo's 600-experiment figures are industry disclosures with no control group and optimise DAU, not learning; the record's own verifier caps industry disclosures at WEAK. Silverman & Barasch 2023 is consumer-behaviour, not ADHD, and its own finding that the boost tracks the DISPLAYED streak rather than the behaviour is the goal-displacement failure mode. ADHD-specific counter-evidence points the other way: Carlson & Tamm 2000 on elevated response-cost/punishment sensitivity plus the abstinence-violation dynamic make a break a plausible practice-abandonment trigger. SPEC §3's 'don't miss twice' rule already captures the useful part with none of the loss framing. NOT OVERTURNED.

### Variable-ratio / intermittent celebration, random reward magnitude, surprise bonuses, 'critical hit' multipliers, mystery unlocks

Rejected in the record on Hulsbosch et al. 2023 (CONFIRMED · MODERATE, 93 ADHD vs 73 TD): the partial-reinforcement extinction effect did NOT replicate in ADHD — participants made fewer responses during extinction, so intermittency did not protect persistence. The gacha/Skinner material argues the schedule works, but Zendle & Cairns isolates RANDOMISATION itself as the harmful ingredient (η² = 0.054 vs 0.004 for non-randomised items), and the ADHD record treats the whole mechanic class as prohibited regardless of currency. Adopted instead: deterministic content-indexed rotation of celebration IDENTITY with certain delivery (visible item 7). NOT OVERTURNED.

### Hidden difficulty rubber-banding (Crash Bandicoot / RE4 pattern) — silently widening the timing window or inflating accuracy after repeated failure

Would corrupt the one thing this app sells: trustworthy measurement. RE4's system is widely resented once discovered precisely because retroactively learning your wins were subsidised deletes them. The correct pattern is Hades' God Mode — visible, opt-in, reversible, sub-JND increments — and the app already implements its structure via the visible `AutoDifficulty` ladder. Hard rule to write into SPEC: adjust the exercise, never the ruler. Never fudge `HitJudge`'s timing window adaptively (note `SessionScreen.kt:181` already widens the window by tempo multiplier — that is a stated, deterministic function of the rung, not a hidden performance-based adjustment, and it must stay that way).

### Energy / lives / stamina / any mechanic that makes him wait to practise, including 'come back tomorrow' framing

Take the session bound, refuse the scarcity. SPEC §3 already bans open-ended sessions and provides a soft ceiling, which is the entire defensible half of the energy-system literature. Manufactured scarcity in a learning tool is indefensible: if he wants to keep playing the piano, the app must never be the thing that stops him. The correct implementation of a session end is 'the structured session is over, here are your numbers, the keyboard still works.'

### Loot-box-style reveal ceremony, anticipation ramp, or a rarity colour hierarchy (grey→green→blue→purple→orange) for performance tiers

Three separate reasons. (1) Ceremony is dead time — Low et al. (in-record, MODERATE) treats uninterruptible celebration as an aversive event with a large subjective effect. (2) Anticipation-building cannot be justified here: the record's Plichta & Scheres anticipation finding is MISCHARACTERIZED · WEAK and the verifier notes the design inference is internally incoherent and a 1.5–4s MID-task construct being applied to multi-hour game timers. (3) The rarity colour lineage is UNVERIFIABLE/WEAK — the claimed Angband→Diablo→WoW chain is largely folklore (Diablo used white/blue/gold; Diablo II used yellow for rare and never used purple), green→blue→purple is a colourblindness trap, and the palette imports a loot-drop connotation. If a tier scale is ever needed, use a single-hue luminance ramp plus a redundant non-colour channel, with the number always visible beside it.

### Screen shake / camera punch on the note highway

The highway is a sight-reading surface. Shaking it displaces the notes he is currently reading, which is the Kao 2020 inverted-U failure mode in its sharpest form — the primary task here is more attention-hungry than aiming in an action game. If any camera punch is used at all, restrict it to the hit-line bar or side chrome, or to moments when reading has stopped (rep end).

### Predictive / optimistic hit-line flash that fires before the MIDI event confirms the note

ITU-R BT.1359-1: audio LEADING video breaks perceptual binding far sooner (45ms) than audio lagging (125ms), because the brain expects sound to arrive after light. The acoustic onset here comes from the real piano at true zero latency and the MIDI event necessarily arrives after it — so Fingerly is structurally in the safe direction already. A predictive renderer would engineer it out of safety, and Powers et al. 2009 says a trained musician's binding window is measurably narrower than a civilian's, so he is the observer most likely to detect it.

### Endowed progress via artificial pre-credit — pre-filling the completion bar, or crediting the count-in / warm-up as a completed segment

Nunes & Drèze 2006 is UNCHECKED and tagged NEEDS_CARE, and the record explicitly flags this family as sitting closest to dark-pattern territory and requiring per-use justification rather than wholesale adoption. It also directly conflicts with the app's core promise of honest measurement — a bar that reads 2/10 when zero work has been done is a false claim. The honest version is adopted instead: the ledger counts real cleared passages, does not reset per chapter, and a returning learner therefore sees genuine accumulated progress.

### Zeigarnik-based UI — deliberately leaving things visibly unfinished on the theory that he will remember them better

The effect does not replicate. A 2025 meta-analysis of 59 publications found a weighted recall ratio of 0.99 across 37 studies — no advantage at all. What survives is the RESUMPTION tendency (Ovsiankina), which is behavioural, not a memory effect, and which SPEC already serves by opening directly into today's session with no decision menu. Any doc text citing Zeigarnik should be corrected to Ovsiankina and downgraded.

### Per-error-class particle colours and shapes (differential-outcomes signalling)

Already rejected at RESEARCH.md:891 — De Meyer et al. is children 8–12 (MISCHARACTERIZED · WEAK) and below the change bar; the render layer's existing 450ms red wrong-key flash already carries the bulk of the identity-bearing signal. Stated here explicitly so the milestone-indexed rotation in visible item 7 is not read as a backdoor reintroduction: that rotation is indexed by WHICH MILESTONE, never by error class.

### Fatigue detector / time-bucketed activity switching when performance degrades

Already rejected at RESEARCH.md:899 on a direct conflict between the two best adult studies (Tucha found significantly steeper deterioration over 20 minutes; Fuermaier tested eight instruments from 6.5 to 33 minutes and found no differential time-on-task effect at all). Invisible mechanic #6's consolidation insert is NOT this — see reconciliations for the explicit distinction.

### Tablet haptics as a reward channel during play

He is touching a piano key, not the tablet. The key's own escapement already provides tactile confirmation at true zero latency, and a tablet buzz would arrive uncorrelated with the finger — Kaaresoja's work shows jitter destroys the sense of agency worse than constant lag does. Reserve haptics for tablet-touch UI (menu, session start/end) where the 5–50ms budget genuinely applies. Note the codebase currently has zero haptic and zero reward-audio wiring, which is correct.

### Cumulative practice minutes / total notes played / session count as a progress or reward metric

Already rejected at RESEARCH.md:887 and reaffirmed here because a boss chapter is exactly where it would be most tempted back in. Bonneville-Roussy & Bouffard: formal practice time explained only ~18% of musical achievement; Duke, Simmons & Cash found retention rankings had no significant relationship with practice time or trial count. Worse, Baker's gaming-the-system work says any countable proxy gets optimised in place of the goal, concentrated precisely on the material understood least. The test to apply to any metric: what is the laziest action that satisfies it, and is that action still worth doing? For minutes-practised the answer is 'play something easy for a long time' — so the metric is wrong.

### Any leaderboard, including a live 'personal best' gauge rendered during a rep

There are no other users, so a social leaderboard is impossible — but the same mechanism reappears as a live comparison against his own past best, which is a leaderboard with one competitor and converts a mastery goal into a performance goal. Hanus & Fox 2015 is CONFIRMED · WEAK (single quasi-experiment, ~80 students, non-random assignment) and cannot drive anything on its own, but Gabay (no live evaluative feedback) and Kluger & DeNisi (self-level feedback consumes the working memory the task needs) already forbid it independently. Past-best comparison AFTER a rep, presented as data, is fine and is what the dashboard's then-vs-now playback already does.

---

## Adversarial critique of the change plan

An independent critic reviewing the plan for gaps, over-engineering, backfires and internal contradictions. Retained because several points are still open work.

# Reward/feedback machinery audit — what actually exists today

## 1. Correct note (hit)

**Single code path:** `NoteHighwayView.kt:259-269`, inside `drainHandler` on `TYPE_NOTE_ON` when `judge.onNoteOn()` returns `idx >= 0`.

| Feedback | Where | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Particle burst | `spawnBurst(noteX[event.data1], keyboardTop, HIT_BURST)` — line 261 | `HIT_BURST = 24` particles, spawned at the key's x on the hit line |
| Key lights green | `pressed[data1] = true` (line 255) → `pressedPaint` in `whiteKeyPaintFor`/`blackKeyPaintFor` (lines 665, 676) | `Color.rgb(0, 230, 118)`, held only while the key is physically down |
| Note rect turns white | `drawNotes` line 590: `HitJudge.STATE_HIT -> hitNotePaint` | `Color.rgb(245, 245, 240)`; visible until `start+duration+200ms < songMs` (`drawFrom` cull, line 558) |
| HUD numbers tick | `rebuildHud()` fires only when `judge.hits + misses + extras` changes (lines 502-506) | `"acc X.X%  hit N  miss N  extra N  combo N"` |
| Wait-mode clock unfreezes | `waitingNow` goes false next frame; hit line alpha resumes pulsing | — |

**Audio on a correct note: none.** `DemoToneSynth` is started only via `setDemoSoundEnabled(true)`, which is called exclusively for `listen`/`autoplay` (`SessionScreen.kt:106`, `HighwayScreen.kt:56`). During real play `recordEnabled = !state.listen` and the synth is off. **Haptics: none anywhere** — a repo-wide grep for `vibrat|haptic|performHapticFeedback|SoundPool|ToneGenerator` returns zero hits.

**No escalation of any kind.** Burst size, color (`particlePaint` = `Color.rgb(0, 230, 118)`), radius (`5f`, line 722), and physics are identical for note 1 and note 500. `judge.combo` and `judge.bestCombo` (`HitJudge.kt:44-45`) are computed but drive **nothing visual** — combo appears only as a decimal in the HUD string (line 753) and end text (line 773). Timing quality (`errMs[best]`, `bestAbs`) is recorded but never fed back per-note — a 1ms hit and a 149ms hit look identical.

## 2. Wrong note (extra)

`NoteHighwayView.kt:270-282`. One thing happens:

- `wrongKeyUntil[data1] = event.timestampNanos + WRONG_FLASH_NANOS` → key drawn with `wrongKeyPaint` = `Color.rgb(229, 57, 53)` for `WRONG_FLASH_NANOS = 450_000_000L` (450 ms), lines 663/674.
- Comment at line 160: *"Red = wrong key, and nothing else"*.
- `judge.extras++`, `combo = 0` (`HitJudge.kt:96-98`) — combo reset is invisible unless `showHud`.
- In wait mode only, the press is logged to `wrongExpectedIdx`/`wrongPlayedNote` (`WRONG_CAPACITY = 256`) for later error classification.

No particles, no sound, no shake, no re-prompt. **Note: a wrong key still lights green via `pressed[]` in the same frame** — `whiteKeyPaintFor` checks `wrongKeyUntil` first (line 663) so red wins, but the ordering is the only thing preventing a green flash on a wrong key.

## 3. Missed note

`HitJudge.advanceTo` (line 112-124) sets `STATE_MISSED`, `misses++`, `combo = 0`. Visual: the falling rect switches to `missNotePaint` = `Color.rgb(95, 100, 106)` (gray, line 208). Comment line 207: *"Missed notes fade to gray — red is reserved for 'wrong key pressed'"*. There is no fade — it's an instant paint swap. Force-miss in wait mode (`forceAdvanceAfterMs = 12_000` ms, `Config` line 51) produces zero visible feedback at all: `judge.forceMiss(waitFrom)` at line 472, then the prompt text just vanishes.

## 4. Reveal (foundations corrective feedback)

At `revealAfterMs = 4000` ms on one prompt (`Config` line 50), `revealedFlag[waitFrom] = true` (line 464). Effect next frame: the falling rect + note name appear (line 584 guard drops), the target key lights `dueRightPaint`/`dueLeftPaint`, and `markLandmarkGroup` tints the anchoring black-key group `landmarkPaint` = `Color.rgb(120, 90, 30)` via `keyDue[n] = 3`. Instant, no transition.

## 5. Drill end (foundations)

`ended = true` (line 486) → `rebuildEndText()` → `onEnded?.invoke(judge)` → `FoundationsScreen.onDrillEnded` sets `runningDrill = null`, which **tears the view down in the same frame**. So `endLine1`/`endLine2` (drawn at lines 548-549) are **never seen in foundations**; the "— tap to restart" suffix (line 774) is also dead there since `tapToRestart = false` (`FoundationsScreen.kt:132`).

What the learner actually gets, on the map screen:
- `summary` string, `FoundationsScreen.kt:103-109`: `"$unaided/${results.size} from memory"` + optional `" · N needed the landmark"` + optional `" · ${median/1000f}s median"` (raw float, e.g. `1.234s`). Plain `Text`, `color = Fg2` (`0xFFC8D2D7`).
- The mastery rows re-render because `version++` (line 111).

No particles, no sound, no animation, no transition. The drill screen simply disappears and a text line appears.

## 6. Mastery map (the only persistent progress surface for basics)

`FoundationsScreen.kt:152-177`, one row per atom (8 atoms: find-C…find-B + `octaves`):
- Prefix: `"✓ "` if `row.mastered`, `"· "` if `row.atCriterion`, `"  "` otherwise (line 156).
- Color: `Mint2 = 0xFF00E676` if mastered, else `Fg2 = 0xFFC8D2D7`.
- Right column, `Dim2 = 0xFF78828C`: `"${hitsToday}/${hitsWanted} today   ${daysCredited}/${daysWanted} days"` + `"   +${rung} oct"` when `rung > 0`.

Thresholds from `FoundationsTrainer.Config`: `sessionCriterionHits = 3`, `postCriterionHitsPerDay = 1`, `criterionDays = 3`, `maxRung = 2`. `mastered()` = `atCriterion() && hitsBeyondHomeOctave() >= 1 && daysCredited >= 3` (line 104).

**Every state transition here is silent.** `daysCredited++` (`FoundationsTrainer.kt:360`) — the single most expensive-to-earn event in the app, requiring a cold correct first attempt on a distinct day — produces no acknowledgement. `mastered()` flipping true just changes a character and a text color on the next recomposition. `songGateOpen()` flipping true writes a SharedPreferences boolean (`FoundationsScreen.kt:112`) with no UI event whatsoever.

## 7. Sitting end (foundations)

`previewDrill()` returns null when no atom has `hitsWantedToday() > 0`. UI, `FoundationsScreen.kt:187-192`: `t.sittingFinishLabel() + ". Nothing left today."` in `Mint2`. Label from `FoundationsTrainer.kt:417-424`: `"${done.size} brought to criterion today: ${labels}"` or `"No keys brought to criterion."`. Static text, replaces the drill button.

## 8. Session end (songs)

`SessionEngine.Phase.VICTORY` → after one attempt, `phase = Phase.DONE` (line 187). `enterVictory()` (line 300) deliberately picks the best-mastered passage one rung *easier* than its best — a designed success moment — but it is presented through the **exact same `BriefingContent`** as every other step; the only difference is the heading string `"Victory lap"` (`SessionScreen.kt:285`), and the generic body text at lines 248-251 is still shown.

`FinishedContent` (`SessionScreen.kt:262-278`): `"Session complete"` headline, then `finishLabel()` in `0xFF00E676`, then `Done` / `Keep going` buttons. Label from `SessionEngine.kt:215-216`: `bestCleanLabel` or `"$totalAttempts reps logged. No clean pass today."`; `bestCleanLabel` format (line 342) = `"Bars X–Y · both hands · 60% tempo · clean ✓"`.

Note `recordFinishCandidate` (line 335) overwrites unconditionally on every `accuracyPercent >= CLEAN_AT` (85f), so the stored label is the **last** clean pass, not the "most impressive" one its comment claims.

Per-attempt feedback between runs is `Diagnosis.of(result)` (`SessionEngine.kt:349-367`), rendered as gray text (`0xFF78828C`) on the next briefing: `"N wrong notes · N missed · timing late avg Nms"`, or `"Clean. N/N notes, avg err Nms."`.

## 9. Progress representations that exist

1. **Drill progress bar** — `drawDrillProgress`, `NoteHighwayView.kt:731-744`. Only drawn when `showHud == false`, i.e. **only in foundations** (`FoundationsScreen.kt:135`). Segmented: `notes.size` rects, bar width `width * 0.5f`, centered, `y = 40f`, height `12f`, `segW - 4f` wide each. Filled `progressBarPaint` (`0,145,75`) for `i < judge.judgedCount()`, else `progressTrackPaint` (`30,38,44`). Fills instantly, no tween.
2. **Live HUD** — `hudLine` + `perfLine` at (24, 48) and (24, 96), `hudPaint` 34px mono. Active in **free play and every song session** (`showHud` defaults to `true` and `SessionScreen` never sets it false). This means the learner sees `"120Hz fps 119  dropped 0  particles 37"` dev telemetry during real practice, and a live falling accuracy percent — precisely the loss-framed evaluative feedback the foundations path deliberately suppresses (comment lines 534-538).
3. **Hit-line beat pulse** — `onDraw` lines 526-529. `beatMs = 60000/tempoBpm`; alpha ramps `255 → 70` across each beat, pinned at `255` while `waitingNow`.
4. **Mastery map** (§6), **dashboard** (`DashboardScreen.kt`: attempts total, due count, hand EMAs, timing bias sentence, per-skill EMA list, session history lines, before/after recording playback buttons), **session finish label**.

There is **no** cross-session streak, no day counter, no total-notes-played number, no session count, no visible ladder position, and no "you are N of M passages through this song" indicator anywhere.

## 10. Particle system — full current capability

Structure-of-arrays pool, `NoteHighwayView.kt:176-183`:
```
px, py, pvx, pvy, plife : FloatArray(MAX_PARTICLES)   // MAX_PARTICLES = 512
particleCount : Int
random = Random(42)                                    // fixed seed, never reseeded
```

**Spawn triggers — exactly two call sites:**
- line 261: `spawnBurst(noteX[note], keyboardTop, HIT_BURST)` on a judged hit. `HIT_BURST = 24`.
- line 495: `perfTestMode` filler, `count = 32` at random x/y until `particleCount >= PERF_TARGET_PARTICLES` (200). Debug only, toggled from `HighwayScreen`.

**Spawn physics** (`spawnBurst`, 686-697): `pvx = (rand-0.5)*900f` (±450 px/s), `pvy = -rand*1100f - 150f` (−150…−1250 px/s, upward), `plife = 0.5f + rand*0.5f` (0.5–1.0 s). Silently drops particles when the pool is full (`while (j < count && particleCount < MAX_PARTICLES)`).

**Update** (`updateParticles`, 699-715): `plife -= dt`; dead particles swap-removed with the tail (no allocation, order not preserved); `pvy += GRAVITY * dt` with `GRAVITY = 1500f`; Euler integration. **`dt` is `frameIntervalNanos / 1e9` (line 493), i.e. the *nominal* refresh period, not the measured frame delta** — so bursts play in slow motion if frames drop rather than staying wall-clock consistent.
